Appearance (in all the above cases):
For the petitioners : Mr. Manoj Kumar Sinha, Advocate
For the SBI : Mr. K. K. Sinha, Advocate
 Mr. Anjani Kr. Mishra, Advocate
=====================================================
CORAM: HONOURABLE MR. JUSTICE VIKASH JAIN
ORAL ORDER
4 31-10-2013 The present batch of petitions are filed alleging deliberate

177
violation of the order dated 14.8.2012 whereby the operation of
the order dated 11.4.2012 impugned therein along with
consequential letters of termination had been stayed during the
pendency of the writ petition.
2. Admittedly, the writ petition has now been finally
decided in terms of order dated 8.8.2013 passed by a Bench of
this Court inter alia, holding that the petitioners had not made out
an exceptional case warranting interference by this Court in
respect of the policy decision taken by the Respondent Bank. It
has been observed that the petitioners had only been engaged as
Business Facilitators and that too, for a period of 12 months with a
rider of termination on giving notice by either side, meaning
thereby, that after expiry of 12 months no right of continuance as
Business Facilitator would continue in favour of the petitioners.
Taking into account the decisions of the Hon’ble Kerala,
Karnataka and Madhya Pradesh High Courts, the writ petition was
finally dismissed.
3. Learned counsel for the petitioner submits that final
disposal of the writ petition would not detract from the fact that
the interim order dated 14.8.2012 was violated and for which the
present petition has been filed. It is further stated that a Review
Petition has also been filed against the final order passed on the

178
writ petition though however, he is unable to supply the Civil
Review number.
4. Having heard the parties and considering that the writ
petition stands dismissed on merits, this Court is not persuaded to
proceed in the matter of contempt against the Opposite Parties,
more so inasmuch as the issue involved disengagement of the
petitioners who had been working on contractual basis under
agreement which had already expired. The interim order was
passed pending filing of a detailed counter affidavit. This Court is
not satisfied that there was any willful disobedience of the Court’s
order.
5. The Contempt petition accordingly stands disposed of.
6. As prayed, the petitioners are granted liberty to make
prayer for revival of the Contempt petitions, if so advised, in the
event that the Review Petition said to have been filed is allowed.
Chandran
 (Vikash Jain, J)
